Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in nucleus theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 35 and the invariant I(A,B) = 430 mod 3.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_35(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 35. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^430(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Given a nucleus algebra A over field F with structural decomposition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 467. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 760/1.

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to nucleus theory, we decompose the problem into 467 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 760. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.
